L1094: ## 9.4 Criteria for Categorizing the Presence of IFD, Adapted from The European Organization for the Research and Treatment of Cancer/Mycoses Study Group Definitions, 2008
L1095: **Proven:** Positive culture from a normally sterile site or microscopy (cytology/histology) showing hyphal forms with evidence of associated tissue damage; OR positive blood culture in the context of a compatible infectious disease process
L1096: **Probable:** At least one host factor, one clinical feature and one mycological criterion
L1097: **Possible:** At least one host factor and one clinical feature
L1098: ## **In detail: Proven Invasive Fungal Disease [Applies to filamentous fungi (molds)]**
L1099: Patients with a positive diagnostic test obtained within the 7 days after the first administration of study medication:
L1100: - Either histopathologic, cytopathologic, or wet mount examination of a needle aspiration or biopsy specimen showing hyphal forms with evidence of associated tissue damage (either microscopically or as an infiltrate or lesion by imaging)
L1101: OR
L1102: - Recovery of a mold by culture from a sample obtained by a sterile procedure from a normally sterile and clinically or radiologically abnormal site consistent with an infectious disease process, e.g., transbronchial biopsy, open-lung biopsy, or brain biopsy.
L1103: OR
L1104: - Blood culture that yields a mold (e.g., *Fusarium* species) in the context of a compatible infectious disease process.
L1105: Host factors and clinical features are not required for patients with proven IFD. However, if such host factors and clinical features are present at baseline, they will be recorded in the eCRF.
L1106: ## **Other Proven Invasive Fungal Disease**
L1107: ## **DEEP TISSUE DISEASE OR FUNGEMIA**
L1108: Non-*Candida* Yeasts
L1109: Histopathologic or cytopathologic examination of a needle aspiration or biopsy specimen from a normally sterile site excluding mucous membranes showing yeast cells;
L1110: OR
L1111: Recovery of a yeast by culture from a sample obtained by a sterile procedure (including a freshly [&lt; 24 hours] placed drain) from a normally sterile and clinically or radiologically abnormal site consistent with an infectious disease process.
L1112: ## CRYPTOCOCCOSIS
L1113: Cryptococcal meningitis documented by a cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) culture positive for *Cryptococcus*. Antigen titers are acceptable only as supportive evidence and for response and outcome assessment. Patients may enroll on the basis of positive India ink or other histopathology diagnostic for *Cryptococcus*.
L1114: ## ENDEMIC FUNGAL DISEASE
L1115: Histoplasmosis, blastomycosis, coccidioidomycosis, paracoccidioidomycosis, and infection due to *Penicillium marneffei*.
L1116: ## Disseminated and/or pulmonary disease
L1117: Must be proven by recovery in culture from a specimen obtained from the affected site, in a host with a temporally related illness consistent with a fungal infectious disease process;
L1118: ## **OR**
L1119: If a culture is negative or not obtained, histopathologic or direct microscopic demonstration of appropriate morphological forms is considered adequate for dimorphic fungi having truly distinctive appearance;
L1120: ## **OR**
L1121: Positive blood culture.
L1122: ## **Additionally:**
L1123: For histoplasmosis, a diagnosis of disseminated disease may be established by
L1124: - a positive *Histoplasma* antigen test on CSF, urine or serum by enzyme immunoassay in sites with access to laboratories where the assays have been validated,
L1125: ## **OR**
L1126: - the presence of characteristic intracellular yeast forms in a peripheral blood smear or in bone marrow.
L1127: For coccidioidomycosis, a diagnosis of disseminated disease may be established by demonstration of coccidioidal antibody in CSF, or a two-dilution rise measured in two consecutive blood samples tested concurrently in the setting of a temporally related infectious disease process.
L1128: ## Probable Invasive Fungal Disease [Applies to filamentous fungi (molds)]
L1129: PLUS
PLUS	At least one host factor (a) as below
At least one clinical feature (b) as below
At least one mycological criterion (c) as below
L1130: ## **a. HOST FACTORS:**
L1131: - Either recently resolved or ongoing neutropenia (neutropenia defined as absolute neutrophil count &lt; 0.5 \times 10^9/L [&lt; 500 / mm^3] for \ge 10 days), temporally related to the onset of fungal disease;
- Or receipt of an allogeneic hematopoietic stem cell transplant (bone marrow transplantation [BMT]);
- Or treatment with other recognized T-cell immune suppressants (such as cyclosporine, tacrolimus, monoclonal antibodies or nucleoside analogues) during the past 90 days (patients whose underlying condition is rheumatologic in nature are not eligible for enrollment);
- Or inherited immunodeficiency (e.g. chronic granulomatous disease, severe combined immunodeficiency).
L1132: ## **b. CLINICAL FEATURES:**
L1133: ## **Lower Respiratory Tract Disease (LRTD)**
L1134: *The medical history must be established to exclude alternative etiologies and to distinguish between an acute or subacute presentation rather than a chronic pulmonary infection. Onset of clinical symptoms and/or physical findings within approximately 2 weeks prior to the first administration of study medication defines an acute or subacute pulmonary infection.*
L1135: The presence of at least one of the following “specific” imaging signs on computed tomography (CT), high resolution computed tomography (HRCT) or magnetic resonance imaging (MRI):
L1136: - Well defined nodule(s) with or without a halo sign
- Air crescent sign
- Cavity
L1137: ## **OR**
L1138: Allogeneic HSCT/BMT or neutropenic patients who have non-specific focal infiltrates, on CT scan (or HRCT) or MRI, must have mycological evidence of disease at the time of study entry and will be classified as probable. Otherwise, patients with non-specific focal infiltrates are not eligible.
L1139: ## **Non-Lower Respiratory Tract Disease (NLRTD)**
L1140: See Table below for a summary of status of invasive fungal disease at baseline for patients with NLRTD.
L1141: ## **Sino-nasal infection:**
L1142: - CT scan or MRI showing **sinusitis PLUS** at least one of the following:Acute localized pain (including pain radiating to eye)
- Nasal ulcer, black eschar
- Extension from the paranasal sinus bony barriers, including into the orbit.
L1143: ## **CNS infection:**
L1144: At least one of the following:
L1145: - Focal lesions on CT or MRI or
- Meningeal enhancement on CT or MRI.
L1146: ## **c. MYCOLOGICAL CRITERIA (cytology, direct microscopy, culture from non-sterile sites, serum)**
L1147: - Either sputum, BAL, bronchial brush samples, or sinus cavity specimen demonstrating the presence of fungal elements either by recovery by culture of a mold or detection by cytology or direct microscopy of hyphal forms;
- Or skin ulcers, draining soft tissue lesions, or fissures for which both microscopy and (fungal) culture are required;

L1217: Kaplan-Meier Survival Plot showing Survival Probability versus Study Day (0 to 90) for two treatment groups: ISAVUCONAZOLE (red line) and VORICONAZOLE (blue line). The curves are nearly superimposed, indicating similar survival probabilities across the study period.
L1218: Forest plot showing Differential All-Cause Mortality at 42 Days by Country (Isavuconazonium-Voriconazole). The Y-axis ranges from -60 to 120. The X-axis lists countries: Bel, Bra, CHN, Deu, Fra, IND, ISR, Kor, RUS, THA, USA, ROW. Points are plotted with error bars (95% CI). Most points cluster near 0, indicating no significant difference. Korea (Kor) shows a positive difference (around 50) with a wide CI, indicating an outlier.
L1219: Kaplan-Meier survival plot showing Survival Probability versus Study Day (0 to 126 days) for Isavuconazole (N=258, solid line) and Voriconazole (N=258, dashed line). The curves are very close, indicating similar survival probabilities over the extended follow-up period.

L1222: This figure displays the Kaplan-Meier survival probability curve for the Isavuconazole treatment group (N=37) over 180 study days. The Y-axis represents Survival Probability (ranging from 0.0 to 1.0) and the X-axis represents Study Day (ranging from 0 to 180). The curve starts at a survival probability of 1.0 at Day 0. The survival probability decreases stepwise, reaching approximately 0.55 by Day 42 and approximately 0.50 by Day 84. The curve remains relatively stable between Day 84 and Day 150, ending at a survival probability of approximately 0.50 by Day 180.

L1224: WSACS003-0702-01 ISAVUCONAZOLE WHITE M 28
Graph showing LFT (ULN) vs Study Day for subject WSACS003-0702-01. The Y-axis (LFT in ULN) ranges from 0 to 15. The X-axis (Study Day) ranges from -5 to 25. The legend indicates: Alanine Aminotransferase (blue squares), Aspartate Aminotransferase (red triangles), Alkaline Phosphatase (pink squares), Bilirubin (black diamonds), and Exposure (red line). Source: Empirica Study 3.1, SDTM Dataset, Study 9766-CL-0103

9766-CL-0104	9709-12	Acute Hepatitis	
58 year old White male with a history of large B-cell lymphoma, chronic lymphocytic leukemia, and unstaged SCC of the lung, being treated for Aspergillus fumigatus pneumonia. Drug discontinued Day 4 due to acute hepatitis (reported Day 5). Days 5-6 ALT and AST rose above 5x ULN. Subject died on Day 6 due to septic shock per investigator. Blood cultures were not positive, and hepatitis serology was not available. Autopsy was not performed. Concomitant medications include acetaminophen. The subject did not have bilirubin values reported, and as such, was not included in the list of subjects that satisfied Hy's Law.

L1241: Figure 11 is a forest plot illustrating the risk assessment of Treatment-Exposure Events (TEAEs) within the >65 year old Controlled Study population. The plot compares Isavuconazonium (ISA, N=63) and Voriconazole (VRC, N=62) across various System Organ Classes (SOCs) and High-Level Group Terms (HLGTs).
The X-axis represents the Odds Ratio (OR) on a logarithmic scale, ranging from 1/32 to 32. The vertical line at OR=1 indicates no difference in risk between the two treatments.
The left panel lists the MedDRA Categories: Adverse Events, detailing the SOC and HLGT for each event.
The right panel displays horizontal lines representing the confidence intervals (CI) for the OR for each event. A point estimate (dot) is shown on the line. If the CI crosses the OR=1 line, the difference is not statistically significant. If the CI is entirely to the right of OR=1, ISA is associated with a higher risk. If the CI is entirely to the left of OR=1, ISA is associated with a lower risk.
Key observations include:
For Eye disorders (HLGT: Vision disorders), ISA shows a lower risk (OR < 1) compared to VRC.
For Gastrointestinal disorders, ISA shows a higher risk (OR > 1) compared to VRC.
For several other categories (e.g., Cardiac disorders, Renal and urinary disorders), the risk difference is not statistically significant.
